0x14010-0x1800F

Why this bank sits where it does

Opens with a 20-entry JMP table at $8000-$803B, the largest of the switchable banks. Nothing else in this bank has been traced.

An address on this page does not identify a byte. $8000-$BFFF is the switchable window, so every address below exists in seven banks at once and only bank 5 has these particular bytes at it. Any address taken from a debugger, a wiki or another disassembly has to carry a bank with it before it means anything — tools/whereis.py --bytes settles which bank really holds a given sequence.

None - every byte in this span is claimed by a region.

Traced code at $8000 code confirmed

bank5.code_8000 · file 0x14010-0x1404B · 60 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $8000-$803B

A run of 60 bytes the code trace reached by following control flow, in bank 5 at $8000-$803B. **This region claims the extent and nothing else.** What the code does has not been established; the evidence is that every byte here is part of an instruction on some path the trace could follow from the interrupt vectors and the banks' entry tables, and that the trace met no illegal opcode anywhere. Generated by tools/gen-code-regions.py from tools/trace-code.py - see that tool's --assumptions for what the reachability rests on. To say more about any part of this stretch, claim it in a hand-authored region file; the generator subtracts what is already claimed before it emits, so the hand-written region wins.

Labyrinth spawn lists: 3 pointers pointer_table lead

bank5.tbl_803C · file 0x1404C-0x14051 · 6 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $803C-$8041

$89FB reads $803C,Y / $803D,Y with Y = 2 * $0478[low nibble of $04C0,X], then (pointer),Y with the high nibble for an object type. Three pointers, $AF61, $AF69, $AF71; the 6 bytes are exactly the gap between the entry table and the code at $8042. How far each list runs is NOT established - the index can reach 15 and the lists are 8 apart.

$803C  61 AF 69 AF 71 AF                                |a.i.q.|

Room shape pointer: $91EA + 18 x (A AND $1F) code confirmed

bank5.code_8042 · file 0x14052-0x14076 · 37 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $8042-$8066

Called once, from $826B. $17:$16 := the address of room shape n, n = A AND $1F: A x 8 + A = 9 A, doubled = 18 A, plus $91EA (the 19 shapes of 18 bytes, one bit per cell, that $8278 lays into the room buffer). RUN: n = 0-31 matched $91EA + 18 n exactly (A = $23 used 3).

Neighbour pointer N ($E0/$E1) code confirmed

bank5.code_8097 · file 0x140A7-0x140B9 · 19 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $8097-$80A9

Unless the row is 0: seek to (row - 1, column) with $80F5 and restore the row. $E1 gets the record's high address byte and $E0 the low; for a row of 0 (no cell to the north) $E1 := 0, which marks 'none'. The five pointers are built in ONE forward walk, in raster order N, W, own, E, S, so the map is read once. The pointer table is $E0 N, $E2 W, $E4 the room itself, $E6 E, $E8 S. RUN.

$8097  A5 18 F0 09 C6 18 20 F5 80 E6 18 A5 17 85 E1 A5  |...... .........|
$80A7  16 85 E0                                         |...|

SEEK: walk the world map to (row $18, column $19) code confirmed

bank5.code_80F5 · file 0x14105-0x14130 · 44 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $80F5-$8120

Continues from the state in $14 (row) / $15 (column) / $17:$16 (record pointer) and stops when ($14, $15) equals ($18, $19). Each step adds the record's length, (first byte AND $0F) + 1, to the pointer; the column counter wraps at 24 and bumps the row. It only goes FORWARD, which is why the neighbours are fetched in raster order. See the world-map notes (24 x 24 records from $8A6E).

$80F5  A0 00 A5 14 C5 18 D0 06 A5 15 C5 19 F0 1D B1 16  |................|
$8105  29 0F 38 65 16 85 16 90 02 E6 17 E6 15 A5 15 C9  |).8e............|
$8115  18 D0 DF A9 00 85 15 E6 14 D0 D7 60              |...........`|

READ THE RECORD (slot $8015): the room kind and the sub-id code confirmed

bank5.code_8121 · file 0x14131-0x1414A · 26 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $8121-$813A

$5D := byte 0 AND $60 (the room kind: 0 plain, $20 item, $40 miniboss, $60 loop gate). Kinds $20 and $40 carry a sub-id: $5E := byte 3 and the field index $10 moves on to 4 ($10 starts at 3). Kinds 0 and $60 leave $5E UNCHANGED (stale from the last room). RUN. Sampled every kind: records [163 39 3 20] -> kind $20, sub-id 20; [67 102 25 30] -> $40, 30; kinds 0 and $60 left $5E alone.

$8121  A9 03 85 10 A0 00 B1 E4 29 60 85 5D F0 0C C9 60  |........)`.]...`|
$8131  F0 08 A0 03 B1 E4 85 5E E6 10                    |.......^..|

Room buffer: tile the structure's pattern code confirmed

bank5.code_815B · file 0x1416B-0x141B3 · 73 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $815B-$81A3

A structure is [list offset][dest][width][height][pattern: width x height ids]. The pattern is copied into the buffer from offset dest and TILED: each row of the pattern repeats across to the end of the 16-cell row, rows are taken in turn, and after `height` rows the pattern starts again, until the buffer is full ($C0). A 1 x 1 structure is a plain floor. $12 is the row's start offset, $1B/$11 the width counter and width, $1A the rows left, $10 the pattern offset. RUN: the real routine built the room buffer for 302 rooms (ordinary rooms and special screens: every map record except the 21 gates and the empty cells) and the buffer equalled the independent model in tools/parse-structs.py in all of them, apart from the key-door cells noted at $82DD.

$816B  B1 18 85 1A C8 84 10 B1 18 9D 40 07 E8 C8 8A 29  |..........@....)|
$817B  0F F0 0D C6 1B D0 F0 A5 11 85 1B A4 10 4C 72 81  |.............Lr.|
$818B  E0 C0 F0 15 A5 12 69 10 85 12 AA C6 1A F0 C8 A5  |......i.........|
$819B  10 18 65 11 85 10 4C 82 81                       |..e...L..|

Draw 16 metatiles: the four tiles code confirmed

bank5.code_83E6 · file 0x143F6-0x14438 · 67 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $83E6-$8428

The first four bytes of a metatile are its tiles top-left, top-right, bottom-left, bottom-right: each is $FF (blank: becomes tile $20) or the byte plus the set's tile base. They go to the band's two tile rows: $0130 + 2 x cell (top row, left and right) and $0150 + 2 x cell (bottom row). The 32-byte rows at $0130 and $0150 are what the NMI (or $E619) copies to the nametable.

Draw 16 metatiles: the attribute quadrant code confirmed

bank5.code_8429 · file 0x14439-0x14466 · 46 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $8429-$8456

The fifth byte: its low three bits pick an attribute pattern in $0199 (00 55 AA FF: the palette repeated in all four quadrants). The attribute byte of the 32 x 32 block ($04E8 + cell / 2; eight per band) is updated for this cell's QUADRANT only: index = column parity + 2 x band parity (top-left, top-right, bottom-left, bottom-right = bits 0-1, 2-3, 4-5, 6-7), the old two bits cleared with the mask $8469[index] and the pattern's two bits ORed in through $846D[index]. Two bands therefore build one attribute row; the second (odd) band completes it. RUN: all six attribute rows ($04E8, 48 bytes) of the labyrinth savestate's room were reproduced exactly.

Clear the status screen's nametable, part 1: its attributes code confirmed

bank5.code_84A9 · file 0x144B9-0x144BB · 3 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $84A9-$84AB

`JSR $C06C` (`$D5CC`) with four inline bytes `40 27 C0 00`: 64 bytes of `00` at PPU `$27C0`, the whole attribute table of nametable 1 (physical `$2400`: the status screen is drawn there, and so is the HUD). Inline argument at $84AC data confirmed

bank5.inline_84AC · file 0x144BC-0x144BF · 4 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $84AC-$84AF

4 bytes of inline argument data following `JSR $D5CC` at bank 5 $84A9 - four-byte inline argument. The callee pops its own return address and reads these bytes through it, so they are data that happens to sit in the instruction stream, and a trace that decodes them produces nonsense that still looks like code. The length is not a guess: it is the constant the callee advances its own pointer by. What the bytes MEAN is not claimed. See annotations/dispatch.json for the routine and for how the class of these routines was enumerated.

Clear the status screen's nametable, part 2: the rows code confirmed

bank5.code_84B0 · file 0x144C0-0x144D2 · 19 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $84B0-$84C2

Palette list: the status screen, 7 palettes and a skip palette confirmed

bank5.pal_84C3 · file 0x144D3-0x144DA · 8 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $84C3-$84CA

A palette list for the walker at bank 7 $D92D (entered at $C05D with the pointer in $18/$19): for list position Y = 0-7 and a value below $FE it far-calls bank 5 $B2E4, which copies palette number A (three colours from the 36 x 3 table at bank 5 $B30A) to $0181 + 3Y; $FE ends the list, $FF skips the position. Positions 0-3 are the four background palettes and 4-7 the four sprite palettes. The list is 05 0F 1F FF 00 01 02 03: background palettes 5, 15 and 31, position 3 skipped (the HUD-colour slot keeps what it had), and sprite palettes 0-3. Read from the code only; there is no status-screen savestate to compare with.

Open: interrupts off, rendering off, save the palette code confirmed

bank5.code_84CB · file 0x144DB-0x144E9 · 15 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $84CB-$84D9

STATUS SCREEN, OPEN (slot `$801B`, called from `$E500`): `SEI` (the DMC-timer IRQ that makes the HUD split is held off; this routine never clears the I flag, the close does), `JSR $C00F` (`$D4C1`: NMI off in `$FF`, rendering off in `$FE`), then the 24 palette bytes `$0181-$0198` are copied to `$04D0-$04E7` (the fade-target buffer, borrowed as a save area; `$0180`, the backdrop, is not saved). The close copies them back. RUN: after open + close the palette RAM equals the one before. RUN: bank 5 live, `$84CB` then `$8711` called on the labyrinth savestate.

$84CB  78 20 0F C0 A2 18 BD 80 01 9D CF 04 CA D0 F7     |x .............|

Open: clear the screen, save sprite 0, hide the sprites code confirmed

bank5.code_84DA · file 0x144EA-0x144F5 · 12 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $84DA-$84E5

`JSR $84A9` clears nametable 1. `$0177` := the Y of sprite 0 (`$0200`, the HUD split's marker, `$BF` in play), which the close puts back. `JSR $C015` (`$D534`) sets all 256 bytes of OAM to `$F8`: every sprite off screen, so the status screen starts with none (the entrance markers and the player's marker are added at the end of the open).

$84DA  20 A9 84 AD 00 02 8D 77 01 20 15 C0              | ......w. ..|

Open: scroll and nametable code confirmed

bank5.code_84E6 · file 0x144F6-0x144FD · 8 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $84E6-$84ED

`$FC` (the vertical scroll shadow) := `$B4` = 180 and `$A2` (the nametable select the NMI writes) := 1. Line 180 of the 240-line nametable is in row 22, so the screen's first visible rows are the bottom rows 28-29 of nametable 1 and then, wrapping, rows 0 on down: that is why the screen is built from rows 28, 29, 0-23 and why the 'KEY' row (29) sits above the key icons (rows 0-1). There is no raster split here (sprite 0 is hidden).

$84E6  A9 B4 85 FC A9 01 85 A2                          |........|

$84EE  A9 C3 85 18 A9 84 85 19 20 5D C0                 |........ ].|

Open: the marker colours, and upload both palettes code confirmed

bank5.code_84F9 · file 0x14509-0x14517 · 15 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $84F9-$8507

`$0196` := `$16` and `$0197` := `$30`: the first two colours of sprite palette 3 (`$0196-$0198`) become red and white (the third, `$0198`, stays `$0F`), the colours of the map's entrance and player markers. `$017F` := 3: upload both background and sprite palettes (bits 0 and 1; see the palette-upload entry in the RAM map). RUN: bank 5 live, `$84CB` then `$8711` called on the labyrinth savestate. Palette RAM after the open ends `... 16 30 0F`.

Open: load the map's quadrant tiles code confirmed

bank5.code_8508 · file 0x14518-0x14524 · 13 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $8508-$8514

`$19:$18` := `$1010`, A := 3, Y := 2, `JSR $C042` (`$D7F9`): arm the decompressor with **block 3 of bank 2** (the block at bank 2 `$959F`, 240 bytes = 15 tiles) for PPU `$1010-$10FF`: background tiles `$01-$0F`. Tile k is the map's quadrant tile: each of its four 4x4-pixel quadrants is filled when the matching bit of k is set (1 top left, 2 top right, 4 bottom left, 8 bottom right), colours 1 and 2, tile 15 solid. The map drawer `$891F` picks the tile by OR-ing those bits. RUN: all 15 tiles decoded from the ROM equal the live CHR after the open, and each tile's four quadrant fills equal its bits. RUN: bank 5 live, `$84CB` then `$8711` called on the labyrinth savestate.

$8508  A9 10 85 19 85 18 A9 03 A0 02 20 42 C0           |.......... B.|

Open: the frame tile (tile $00) code confirmed

bank5.code_8515 · file 0x14525-0x14527 · 3 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $8515-$8517

`JSR $C06F` with a 19-byte inline PPU string `10 10 00` + 16 bytes: tile `$00` of pattern table 1 (PPU `$1000`) := plane 0 all `00`, plane 1 `FF 80 80 80 80 80 80 80`: colour 2 along the top row and the left column. It is the map frame's line tile (and is also the first key-door picture's tile `$00`: the close rebuilds that).

$8515  20 6F C0                                         | o.|

Queued PPU write at $8518 data confirmed

bank5.inline_8518 · file 0x14528-0x1453A · 19 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $8518-$852A

A queued PPU write item, written inline after `JSR $D576` at bank 5 $8515. Its shape is [count][ppu_hi][ppu_lo][count characters]: the destination $1000 is in the block itself. The block is 19 bytes and the caller resumes immediately after it; the length comes from the first byte by the rule in $D591 - (byte & $7F) + 3 below $C0, 9 at or above - and $D5A5 advances the routine's own pointer by exactly that. The 16 payload bytes are '................' read as ASCII, which is not text - so they are tile numbers for something drawn rather than written, and this region is kind data on that ground alone. Characters are ASCII for the letters and space. NOT ESTABLISHED: the glyphs for codes outside A-Z and space, which only the font can settle - and no font has been found yet, because CHR is RAM on this cartridge.

$8518  10 10 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 FF 80 80 80 80  |................|
$8528  80 80 80                                         |...|

bank5.code_853F · file 0x1454F-0x14559 · 11 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $853F-$8549

Far-call argument at $854A data confirmed

bank5.inline_854A · file 0x1455A-0x1455C · 3 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $854A-$854C

Three bytes of far-call argument following `JSR $D76F` at bank 5 $8547 - bank number, then the target address minus one, little-endian. They select **bank 2 $8003**. The stored word is $8002 rather than $8003 because $D790 pushes these two bytes as a fake return address and lets RTS add the one back; that is not an inference, it is what the four instructions at $D799-$D7A4 do. A far call is the one reference in this ROM that no grep for an operand will ever surface, so the target is recorded here where a reader will find it.

$854A  02 02 80                                         |...|

bank5.code_858B · file 0x1459B-0x145A5 · 11 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $858B-$8595

`$16` walks the 20 slots of the table `$8690` in steps of 4 (Y = `$16`): slots 0-6 are the keys, slot 7 (Y = `$1C`) is the zero slot and is skipped, slots 8-19 are the 12 weapons. Each slot: the cell to draw at `[ppu lo][ppu hi]`, its attribute address and its tile.

Open: a weapon slot - its level code confirmed

bank5.code_85AC · file 0x145BC-0x145D0 · 21 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $85AC-$85C0

Y above `$1C`: weapon number := Y/4 - 8 (0-11; the `LSR`s leave carry clear, so the `SBC #$07` subtracts 8), `JSR $C075` (`$E498`: the 2-bit level of that weapon from `$04AC-$04AE`, 0-3, 0 = not owned); pushed. The first `$18`/`$19` stored here (the slot address with the row ORed with `$20`) are overwritten at `$85C7`: dead stores.

Open: draw the slot's picture code confirmed

bank5.code_85C1 · file 0x145D1-0x145E9 · 25 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $85C1-$85D9

A (the key flag, or the weapon level) indexes `$85DA`: `$17` := `00 00 55 AA` = the attribute pattern mask (keys always 0; weapons level 0-1 palette 0, level 2 palette 1, level 3 palette 2). `$18`/`$19` := the slot's PPU address. The pushed value is pulled: zero (key not held, weapon not owned) draws tile `$20`, the blank; otherwise the slot's own tile (`$8693,Y`).

$85C1  A8 B9 DA 85 85 17 A4 16 B9 90 86 85 18 B9 91 86  |................|

Icon attribute by weapon level: 00 00 55 AA data confirmed

bank5.tbl_85DA · file 0x145EA-0x145ED · 4 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $85DA-$85DD

Read by $85C2 LDA $85DA,Y with Y = 0 or 1 for a key (held or not) and 0-3 for a weapon (its level from $C075), giving the attribute byte that picks the icon's palette: level 2 gets palette 1, level 3 palette 2. CORRECTION: the trace decoded $85DA as a BRK. It is never executed - the instruction before it is LDA #$20 / BNE, which always branches.

$85DA  00 00 55 AA                                      |..U.|

Open: draw one 16x16 cell and step to the next slot code confirmed

bank5.code_85DE · file 0x145EE-0x14606 · 25 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $85DE-$85F6

Tile in A, the slot's attribute address (`$8692,Y`) in Y, `JSR $C054` (`$D865`): queue a 2x2 cell at PPU `$19:$18` with that tile as base, the attribute byte at the given address updated with the pattern mask `$17`. The four tiles go in the order base, base+2 (top row) and base+1, base+3 (bottom). Then `$16` += 4; at `$50` (20 slots) the loop ends, else `JMP $858D`. RUN: with weapon levels `$04AC` = `E4`, `$04AD` = `1B`, `$04AE` = `C1` the owned weapons showed their slot tile and the others blank. RUN: bank 5 live, `$84CB` then `$8711` called on the labyrinth savestate.

$85DE  B9 93 86 48 B9 92 86 A8 68 20 54 C0 A5 16 18 69  |...H....h T....i|
$85EE  04 A8 C0 50 F0 03 4C 8D 85                       |...P..L..|

Open: start the cursor repeat counter, blink the marker code confirmed

bank5.code_867F · file 0x1468F-0x14699 · 11 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $867F-$8689

`$15` := 5 and `$14` := 0, then a far call (inline `02 1D 80`) to bank 2 `$801E` (-> `$8998`), the blinking player marker (it writes the last four sprites, OAM `$02F0-$02FF`, one pattern per call; it reads neither `$14` nor `$15`). **CORRECTION: the 5 and 0 are not arguments of `$8998` (the first version of this note called them a lead): `$15` is the cursor handler's auto-repeat counter, read only by `$8946`, so a direction held from before the screen opened waits 5 frames; `$14` is not read by the handler.** The marker sits at (column x 4 + `$10`, row x 4 + `$3B`) = (`$30`, `$6B`) for position (8, 12).

Far-call argument at $868A data confirmed

bank5.inline_868A · file 0x1469A-0x1469C · 3 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $868A-$868C

Three bytes of far-call argument following `JSR $D76F` at bank 5 $8687 - bank number, then the target address minus one, little-endian. They select **bank 2 $801E**. The stored word is $801D rather than $801E because $D790 pushes these two bytes as a fake return address and lets RTS add the one back; that is not an inference, it is what the four instructions at $D799-$D7A4 do. A far call is the one reference in this ROM that no grep for an operand will ever surface, so the target is recorded here where a reader will find it.

Queued PPU write at $86E3 data confirmed

bank5.inline_86E3 · file 0x146F3-0x146FB · 9 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $86E3-$86EB

A queued PPU write item, written inline after `JSR $D576` at bank 5 $86E0. Its shape is [count][ppu_hi][ppu_lo][count characters]: the destination $3C20 is in the block itself. The block is 9 bytes and the caller resumes immediately after it; the length comes from the first byte by the rule in $D591 - (byte & $7F) + 3 below $C0, 9 at or above - and $D5A5 advances the routine's own pointer by exactly that. The 6 payload bytes are ' ...' read as ASCII, which is not text - so they are tile numbers for something drawn rather than written, and this region is kind data on that ground alone. The count byte is $C7, at or above $C0, so its length is the fixed 9 rather than a character count - the one branch of $D591 that does not derive the length from the byte. Characters are ASCII for the letters and space. NOT ESTABLISHED: the glyphs for codes outside A-Z and space, which only the font can settle - and no font has been found yet, because CHR is RAM on this cartridge.

$86E3  C7 3C 20 20 20 20 F7 00 FF                       |.<    ...|

Far-call argument at $89C8 data confirmed

bank5.inline_89C8 · file 0x149D8-0x149DA · 3 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $89C8-$89CA

Three bytes of far-call argument following `JSR $D76F` at bank 5 $89C5 - bank number, then the target address minus one, little-endian. They select **bank 2 $801E**. The stored word is $801D rather than $801E because $D790 pushes these two bytes as a fake return address and lets RTS add the one back; that is not an inference, it is what the four instructions at $D799-$D7A4 do. A far call is the one reference in this ROM that no grep for an operand will ever surface, so the target is recorded here where a reader will find it.

$89C8  02 1D 80                                         |...|

World map: 24 x 24 rooms level confirmed

bank5.map_8A6E · file 0x14A7E-0x151F9 · 1,916 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $8A6E-$91E9

576 variable-length records, 24 rows of 24 - one per room of the labyrinth. A record is (first byte AND $0F) + 1 bytes ($8103: LDA ($16),Y / AND #$0F / SEC / ADC $16). $80F5 seeks through it; $8071 walks it as the player moves ($52 column, $53 row); $882D draws it on the status screen as 2x2-cell background tiles, sprites marking corridor entrances only. BYTE 0: low nibble length-1, $80 exactly empty, bit 4 = layout pointer present, bits 5-6 = room kind ($5D; $20 and $40 carry a sub-id). BYTE 1: low three bits = the KEY that reveals the cell ($04B0), bit 3 = the room has enemies, bits 7/6/5/4 = openings north/west/east/south ($82DD against the neighbours $E0/$E2/$E6/$E8), and byte 0 bit 7 puts a key door in an opening. BYTE 2: bit 7 = corridor entrance, low seven bits = corridor number. THEN, in order: sub-id ($5E), spawn script id ($57, into $A4C7), layout pointer. CHECKS: 370 of 370 non-empty records are exactly as long as their flags imply; corridor numbers 1-21 occur once each; in the labyrinth savestate the live room pointer $E4 = $8E4C is this walk's row 12 column 8, and that room's layout and openings match the nametable cell for cell; 211 rooms with enemies use script ids 1-47, 47 distinct. CORRECTION: this region was first 807 records running to $960E, proved by 'the walk lands exactly on $960F' - a weak test that a walk over arbitrary data passes about one time in four. The map ends at $91EA, which $8042 names as the room-shape base. Generated by tools/parse-map.py.

$8A6E  02 36 09 36 6E 09 08 2A AE 94 43 66 19 1E 03 5E  |.6.6n..*..Cf...^|
$8A7E  09 28 80 80 80 80 A3 27 03 14 03 7F 0A 25 15 5F  |.(.....'.....%._|
$8A8E  0A 29 8C 94 82 17 8A 43 37 1A 20 03 6F 0A 2E 14  |.).....C7. .o...|
$8A9E  57 0A 46 94 80 36 1B 04 37 17 C9 94 02 13 04 A3  |W.F..6..7.......|
$8AAE  23 02 41 14 73 04 6F 95 82 43 84 80 80 80 02 96  |#.A.s.o..C......|
$8ABE  09 80 15 3E 09 27 9B 95 14 D6 09 46 94 80 80 80  |...>.'.....F....|
$8ACE  80 82 27 01 14 D7 0A 15 94 03 9F 0A 2D 15 AF 0A  |..'.........-...|
$8ADE  2F 85 95 02 C7 0A 03 3F 0A 2D 03 CF 0A 03 80 15  |/......?.-......|

bank5.level_9340 · file 0x15350-0x1561E · 719 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $9340-$960E

79 room layouts, read by $8243 through the pointer at the end of a map record. [shape | count << 5][fill tile], then count x [tile][position][six-bit mask | direction]: $82A3 walks the mask along a step from $82D9 (-15, 1, 16, 17 - up-right, right, down, down-right), writing the tile at each set bit. Dialogue message pointer table pointer_table confirmed

bank5.msgtable_960F · file 0x1561F-0x15648 · 42 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $960F-$9638

21 little-endian pointers, $960F-$9638, into the dialogue script that follows immediately. Read by bank 5 $8476: LDA $0620,X gives an object's message number, ASL A doubles it into Y, and LDA $960F,Y / LDA $9610,Y fetch the two halves - so the base in the operand is this region's own address, which is the strongest statement available that the table starts here. The entry count is bounded by structure, not by the reader: 21 x 2 = 42 bytes ends at $9639, exactly where the first message begins, with nothing over. NOTHING IN THE CODE BOUNDS IT - the index comes from $0620,X, an object field, and no CPY or mask on it has been found. Slots pointing at the same message: $9BC8 is named twice. That is in the data, not a parse error. Every one of the pointers lands on a message head, and the messages partition the block end to end - two statements from two directions, neither derived from the other.

$960F  39 96 B3 98 B6 99 13 9B 6F 9B C8 9B C8 9B D1 9B  |9.......o.......|
$961F  06 9C 2C 9C 38 9C 43 9C 90 9C C8 9C 2C 9D A3 9D  |..,.8.C.....,...|
$962F  E2 9D 26 9E 7D 9E B5 9E E4 9E                    |..&.}.....|

"If someone is reading this... I must have failed..." text confirmed

bank5.message_9639 · file 0x15649-0x158C2 · 634 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $9639-$98B2

Dialogue message, 634 bytes, named by table slot 0. It reads: If someone is reading this... I must have failed. This star "NAJU" was our home, but we were invaded by evil life-forms. Inline jump table B (10 entries) pointer_table confirmed

bank5.tbl_A1C4 · file 0x161D4-0x161E7 · 20 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $A1C4-$A1D7

Ten entries, each a handler address minus one, inline after the JSR at $A1C1. THE COUNT COMES FROM THE CALLER, which is what this project prefers: $A1A2 does LDA ($E4),Y / AND #$7F / SEC / SBC #$01 / STA $15 / CMP #$0A / BCC $A1B7, and the failing side takes an error path at $A1AA. So the index is 0 to 9 and can be nothing else. Two independent checks agree: entry 2 targets $A1D8, exactly where a tenth entry ends, and after seeding all ten the trace leaves this region as an unclaimed gap of exactly 20 bytes.

Item or shop stock, by map sub-id data confirmed

bank5.tbl_A377 · file 0x16387-0x163CA · 68 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $A377-$A3BA

$814E: LDY $5E / LDA $A377,Y / STA $55, with $5E the sub-id a map record of kind $20 or $40 carries (tools/tgl/worldmap.py). The map uses sub-ids 0-67 and this stretch is exactly 68 bytes, bounded by code on both sides. Values 0-$11. $55 is an ITEM: in an item room (kind $20, byte 0 bit 7 clear) the type-$31 object spawned on the layout's metatile 6 takes its icon from $55 (bank 2 $84EE), so this is the item, 0-17 = the 18 icons. In the shop screens it is instead an index: screen 6 reads $A067 + 3 x $55, screen 2 $A04E + 5 x $55 - which is why sub-ids 58-67 hold 0-4. CORRECTION to 'what $55 selects is NOT established'. docs/labyrinth.html#items lists every room.

$A377  00 0D 0B 0B 0D 11 0F 0C 0B 0C 0C 03 0D 05 11 0E  |................|

ROOM SPECIAL CASES (slot $8030): which kind of room? code confirmed

bank5.code_A3BB · file 0x163CB-0x163D4 · 10 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $A3BB-$A3C4

Bank 5 slot $8030, called by the room loaders ($EBC3, $EF00) after the room buffer is built. $56 := $FF (no pick-up box open). The room kind $5D (from the map record's byte 0 bits 5-6): $20 = an item room -> $A3C5, $60 = a loop gate -> $A3DF, anything else returns. (Kind $40, the miniboss, is handled by the room-script setup $A462.)

$A3DB  C9 60 D0 FB                                      |.`..|

Loop gate: does a recent stretch of the move history cancel out? code confirmed

bank5.code_A3DF · file 0x163EF-0x16414 · 38 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $A3DF-$A404

Walks the move history $04F0-$04F9 from the NEWEST move back (X = 0-9), adding each move's displacement from the table at $A41B into two separate 8-bit sums: $15 = x (west $FF, east 1) and $14 = y (north $FF, south 1); code 4 (no move) adds nothing. After each addition, if both sums are zero the player is back where that stretch of moves began: -> $A405. After all ten without a zero it returns. RUN: E then W opened it; E N W S (a square) opened it; E E N S W, N N S E W W and ten E did not; a history of all 4s opens it at once.

$A3FF  E0 0A D0 E4 F0 D5                                |......|

Move displacements: x low byte, y high byte, by exit code data confirmed

bank5.tbl_A41B · file 0x1642B-0x16434 · 10 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $A41B-$A424

Read as [$A41B + code] = x and [$A41C + code] = y for the exit codes 0 N, 2 W, 4 none, 6 E, 8 S: N (0, $FF), W ($FF, 0), none (0, 0), E (1, 0), S (0, 1). CORRECTION: this was claimed as a 1-byte and a 9-byte table with no meaning; and the loop-gate note said the moves 'sum to zero', which hides that it is a two-dimensional displacement tested after every step.

bank5.code_A663 · file 0x16673-0x1667A · 8 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $A663-$A66A

Reached by JMP from the room enemy set-up ($A46B) when the room kind $5D is $40. $5E = the record's sub-id, which is also the miniboss's FLAG number in the array at $04A0; JSR $C0E1 (-> bank 7 $EFE0) tests it. DRAW ONE ITEM: header code confirmed

bank5.code_A6EB · file 0x166FB-0x16709 · 15 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $A6EB-$A6F9

In: $19:$18 = a base address and Y = the offset of an ITEM [dest][width][height][tiles] (or, from the room builder, the item list's pointer with Y = 0). $10 and X := dest (the buffer offset of the item's top-left cell); $13 := the width byte (bit 7 = ONE tile repeated across the width) and $11 := width AND $7F. Draw one item: one row code confirmed

bank5.code_A71B · file 0x1672B-0x1673B · 17 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $A71B-$A72B

Each tile: a non-zero tile is stored at $0740 + X, ZERO IS TRANSPARENT (the cell keeps what is already there); Y advances unless the width byte had bit 7 set (the same tile again); X advances; until `width` cells are done. A width that runs past column 15 simply carries on into the next buffer row: there is no clipping.

bank5.tbl_A7CA · file 0x167DA-0x167F1 · 24 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $A7CA-$A7E1

Palettes: 36 x 3 colours palette confirmed

bank5.pal_B30A · file 0x1731A-0x17385 · 108 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $B30A-$B375

Thirty-six palettes of three colours. $B2E4 is the reader and it states the layout exactly: ASL A / ADC $11 / TAX makes X = A * 3, then LDA $B30A,X / LDA $B30B,X / LDA $B30C,X go to $0181,Y / $0182,Y / $0183,Y with Y = the destination index * 3. $0181 is the palette staging area the NMI uploads - $DEF3 walks it reading $0180 for the shared backdrop and $0181,Y upward for the rest - so three bytes here are colours 1, 2 and 3 of a palette and colour 0 comes from $0180. THE EXTENT: $B2E4 is reached through $D92D (entry $C05D), which walks a list of up to eight palette indices ($FE ends it, $FF skips a slot, CPY #$08); every such list in the ROM - the scene records at bank 5 $AE50, the status screen's, and every bank 4 command-4 list - uses indices 0-35 and nothing higher, and 36 x 3 bytes end exactly where set record 6 begins at $B376. CORRECTION: this region was 37 palettes, bounded by 'every byte is a legal colour for exactly 111 bytes'; the 37th 'palette' was the first three bytes of set record 6 (07 11 00), which happen to be legal colours. HARDWARE: two savestates hold exactly records 0-3 in sprite palette RAM. Which palette is used where is in tools/tgl/scene.py.

background113121111101162212161C0C16302A27

Colours are an approximation. The NES makes colour as an analogue NTSC signal, not from a table, so no set of RGB values is the right one and every emulator ships a different one. The hex value in each swatch is the byte in the ROM; that part is exact.

Bank 5 identity byte data confirmed

bank5.bank_id_BFFF · file 0x1800F-0x1800F · 1 bytes · PRG bank 5 · CPU $BFFF-$BFFF

One byte at $BFFF, the last byte of PRG bank 5, holding the bank's own number. Banks 0 to 6 each carry one; bank 7 does not, because bank 7's last byte is at $FFFF and belongs to the IRQ vector. These seven bytes are the ROM half of a boot-time mapper self-test. $FF23-$FF43, reached from the reset handler, is a loop over Y = 6 down to 0: TYA / JSR $D705 selects the bank in Y, CPY $BFFF then compares Y against the byte this region holds, and BEQ $FF41 falls through to DEY only when the two agree. If they do not, LDA $FF3A,Y fetches a per-bank code, stores it at $0180, calls $DED2 and then spins forever at JMP $FF37. So the game refuses to start unless every one of the seven switchable banks can be selected and answers with its own number - which is the check a UxROM board needs, because a bus conflict on the latch fails silently and leaves the wrong bank mapped. Reading the byte at $BFFF works no matter which bank is live precisely because every bank has one at the same address and they all differ. NOT ESTABLISHED: what $DED2 does with $0180, and therefore whether the failure is displayed or merely hangs.

$BFFF  05                                               |.|
